as we explain now.
Here we use the functors from
(
4.6
) and (
4.7
).

We consider a morphism
\phi\colon{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}})
. In the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
we in addition assume that it belongs to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{eadd,ndeg}})
.

We first observe, using that the restriction of
\mathbf{M}\phi
to
{\mathbf{C}}
is precisely
\phi
itself, that if
(C,\rho,\mu)
is in
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)
or
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G}(X)
, then
(\phi(C),\mathbf{M}\phi(\rho),\mathbf{M}\phi(\mu))
is in
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)
or
{\mathbf{D}}^{G}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
.

If
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is a morphism in
\mathbf{C}^{G}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)
which is
U
-controlled, then

\mathbf{M}\phi(A)\colon(\phi(C),\mathbf{M}\phi(\rho),\mathbf{M}\phi(\mu))\to(\phi(C^{\prime}),\mathbf{M}\phi(\rho^{\prime}),\mathbf{M}\phi(\mu^{\prime}))
is also
U
-controlled.
It follows that
\phi^{G}(X)
in (
4.5
) induces by restriction functors

\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to{\mathbf{D}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)\,,\quad\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to{\mathbf{D}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\,.

Consequently,
\phi^{G}(X)
also preserves the completions and restricts to
functors

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{D}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)\,,\quad\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{D}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\,.

In conclusion, we actually have constructed functors

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}})\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,,\quad(X,{\mathbf{C}})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

(5.5)

and

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{eadd,ndeg}})\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,,\quad(X,{\mathbf{C}})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\,.

(5.6)

6
Properties of the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}

In Section
5
we introduced the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
from

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
.
These functors are an intermediate step towards our desired coarse homology theories, which will be obtained by composing them with homological functors, see Definition
3.23
. In the present section we prepare the verification of the axioms of a coarse homology theory by verifying similar properties for
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
. Note that the target of these functors
is not stable. So in particular the formulation of the corresponding excision
property in Lemma
6.10
is not yet exactly the property described in Definition
2.27
.

We fix
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and adopt Assumption
5.1
. Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. Recall Definition
3.15
of an additive
C^{*}
-category.

Lemma 6.1
.

If
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is additive, then
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
are additive.

Proof.

We consider the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
The orthogonal sum of two objects
(C,\rho,\mu)
and
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

is given by
(C\oplus C^{\prime},\rho\oplus\rho^{\prime},\mu\oplus\mu^{\prime})
, where
(C\oplus C^{\prime},(e,e^{\prime}))
is an orthogonal sum of the family
(C,C^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
which exists by our assumption. In order to see that this sum is small note that
(\mu\oplus\mu^{\prime})(B)=e\mu(B)e^{*}+e^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}(B)e^{\prime,*}
. Since

{\mathbf{C}}
is an ideal in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
and
\mu(B)
,
\mu^{\prime}(B)
belong to
{\mathbf{C}}
by the smallness of the summands, we have
(\mu\oplus\mu^{\prime})(B)\in{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C\oplus C^{\prime})
for every
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}_{X}
.

The argument for
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is similar. One checks in a straightforward manner that if
both summands are locally finite, then so is the sum.
∎

The functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
are not coarsely invariant except in degenerate cases.
But the following lemma shows that they are coarsely invariant (see Definition
2.26
) if we consider them as functors with values in the
(2,1)
-category
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
of unital
C^{*}
-categories, unital functors and unitary isomorphisms.

Lemma 6.2
.

The functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
send pairs of close morphisms (Definition
2.16
) to pairs of unitarily isomorphic morphisms.

Proof.

Let
f,f^{\prime}\colon X\to Y
be two morphisms in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
which are close to each other. Then we define a
unitary isomorphism of functors

u\colon f_{*}\to f^{\prime}_{*}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)\,,\quad u=(u_{(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))}

such that
u_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\colon(C,\rho,f_{*}\mu)\to(C,\rho,f^{\prime}_{*}\mu)
is given by the identity of the object
C
considered in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.
We observe that
this morphism is equivariant, unitary, and controlled by the entourage
(f^{\prime},f)({\mathtt{diag}}(X))
of
Y
. Since
f
and
f^{\prime}
are close to each other, this entourage belongs to
{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}
and we can conclude that
u_{(C,\rho,\mu)}
is controlled.

The argument for
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
is the same.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

The functors

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
are
u
-continuous (Definition
2.28
).

Proof.

We consider the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
. The argument for
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
is exactly the same.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.
Then we must show that the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

(6.1)

is an isomorphism.
For every entourage
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}

the canonical morphism

i_{U}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

is the identity on the level of objects and an inclusion of a subspace on the level of morphisms.
By
[
Bun
, Lem. 4.7]
we know that
the colimit in (
6.1
) is realized by the wide subcategory of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
whose morphism spaces are given by

\overline{\bigcup_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U})}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\,.

Since this space contains the subspace

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))
which is dense in

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))
by definition,
we conclude that (
6.1
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Recall Definition
3.22
of the notion of a flasque
C^{*}
-category, and Definition
2.20
of a flasque
G
-bornological coarse spaces.

Lemma 6.4
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits countable AV-sums, then the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
send flasque
G
-bornological coarse spaces to flasque
C^{*}
-categories.

Proof.

We first consider the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
.
Note that finite AV-sums in a
C^{*}
-category are the same thing as sums in its multiplier category. Therefore our assumption on
{\mathbf{C}}
implies that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is additive.
It follows from Lemma
6.1
that

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is additive.

Assume that
X
is flasque and that flasqueness is implemented by
f\colon X\to X
. We define an endofunctor

S\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
as follows:

1.

objects: The functor
S
sends an object
(C,\rho,\mu)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to

S(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq\big(\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C,\oplus_{{\rm Nature}}\rho,\oplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}f^{n}_{*}\mu\big)\,.

Note that this involves a choice of a representative of an orthogonal AV-sum
(\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C,(e_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}})
which exists by the assumption on
{\mathbf{C}}
. We use
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
in order to see that

\oplus_{{\rm Nature}}\rho
and
\oplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}f^{n}_{*}\mu
are well-defined.

2.

morphisms: The functor
S
sends a morphism
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to
S(A)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}A\colon S(C,\rho,\mu)\to S(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, where we again use

[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
in order to see that
\oplus_{{\rm Nature}}A
is a well-defined multiplier morphism.

It is easy to see that
S(C,\rho,\mu)
satisfies the conditions listed in Definition
4.3
. In order check that
S(C,\rho,\mu)
is small (see Definition
5.2
), consider
B
be in
{\mathcal{B}}_{X}
. By Condition
2.20
.
3
there exists an
n_{0}
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
f^{n_{0}+1}(X)\cap B=\emptyset
. We then have

(\oplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}f^{n}_{*}\mu)(B)=\oplus_{n=0}^{n_{0}}\mu((f^{n})^{-1}(B))\oplus(\oplus_{n\geq n_{0}+1}0)\,.

Note that
\oplus_{n=0}^{n_{0}}\mu((f^{n})^{-1}(B))
is a short-hand notation for
\sum_{n=0}^{n_{0}}e_{n}\mu((f^{n})^{-1}(B))e_{n}^{*}
. Since
(f^{n})^{-1}(B)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{X}

by properness of
f
and since
(C,\rho,\mu)
is small, we have
\mu((f^{n})^{-1}(B))\in{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
. Since
{\mathbf{C}}
is an ideal in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
, we have
e_{n}\mu((f^{n})^{-1}(B))e_{n}^{*}\in{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C)
. It follows that
\oplus_{n=0}^{n_{0}}\mu(f^{-1}(B))\in{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}C)
.

We now assume that
A
is
U
-controlled. Then one can check similarly as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
that

S(A)
is
V
-controlled for
V\coloneqq\bigcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}(f^{n}\times f^{n})(U)
. Since
V\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
by
Condition
2.20
.
2
,
we see that

S(A)
is controlled.
By continuity of the functor
S
on morphism spaces, if
A
can be approximated in norm by invariant controlled morphisms, then
S(A)
has the same property.

We can adopt the choice for the sum of functors on the left-hand side in the following equation such that

{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}\oplus(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)\circ S)=S\,.

(6.2)

Again by assumption on
f
we know that
f
is close to
{\mathtt{id}}_{X}
.
From Lemma
6.2
we conclude that
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)
is unitarily isomorphic to
{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}
.
Therefore
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)\circ S
is unitarily isomorphic to
S
, and hence
{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}\oplus S
is unitarily isomorphic to
S
.

In the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
we show by an inspection of the construction that the functor
S
preserves locally finite objects. ∎

We now start working towards excisiveness of the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
which will be stated in a precise way in Lemma
6.10
.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let
Y
be a
G
-invariant subset of
X
.

Definition 6.5
.

We define the wide subcategory

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
with the spaces of morphisms given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\coloneqq\mu^{\prime}(Y){\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\mu(Y)\,.

The
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
is unital with the identity
\mu(Y)
on the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
. But in general the inclusion
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is only a morphism in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
The inclusion
Y\to X
induces a morphism

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)

(6.3)

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
.
It is injective on objects and fully faithful.
Similarly we get a morphism

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)\,.

(6.4)

Lemma 6.6
.

1.

If
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete, then (
6.3
) is an unitary equivalence.

2.

The functor (
6.4
) is a unitary equivalence.

Proof.

We give the argument for (
6.3
). The case of (
6.4
) is analoguous.
It
is similar to a part of the proof of
[
BE20b
, Prop. 8.82]
.
Let us denote the functor (
6.3
) by

i\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)\,.

We construct an inverse functor

q\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)

as follows:

1.

objects: The functor
q
sends an object
(C,\rho,\mu)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
to the object
q(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
, where
(C^{\prime},e)
is a choice of a representative of the image of the projection
\mu(Y)
which exists
since we assume that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete.
In the case of (
6.4
) the existence of
(C^{\prime},e)
already follows
from the standing hypothesis of effective additivity.

2.

morphisms: Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
be a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
. Then we define
q(A)\coloneqq\tilde{e}^{*}Ae\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(\tilde{C}^{\prime},\tilde{e}^{*}\tilde{\rho}\tilde{e},\tilde{e}^{*}\tilde{\mu}\tilde{e})
, where
(C^{\prime},e)
and
(\tilde{C}^{\prime},\tilde{e})
are the choices of the representatives for the images of
\mu(Y)
and
\tilde{\mu}(Y)
made above.

Above we have used a shortend notation. More precisely,
e^{*}\rho e
is the family of morphisms
({(ge)}^{*}\rho_{g}e\colon C^{\prime}\to gC^{\prime})_{g\in G}

and
e^{*}\mu e
is the function which sends
Z
in
\mathcal{P}_{Y}
to the projection
e^{*}\mu(Z)e
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(C^{\prime})
.
It is straightforward to verify that
q
is well-defined.
The definition of
q
on morphisms extends to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
,
but this might not give a functor since it is then not necessarily compatible with the composition of morphisms.

We now construct the unitary isomorphism

u\colon q\circ i\to{\mathtt{id}}
.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
. Assume that

(C^{\prime},e)
is the chosen representative of the image of
\mu(Y)
in the construction of
q
. In this case
\mu(Y)={\mathtt{id}}_{C}
and
e
is a unitary isomorphism. We set

u_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq e\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(C,\rho,\mu)\,.

It is straightforward to check that
u
is natural.

Finally, we construct a unitary isomorphism
v\colon i\circ q\to{\mathtt{id}}
.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
.
Let
(C^{\prime},e)
be the representative of the image of
\mu(Y)
in the construction of
q
. Then

(i\circ q)(C,\rho,\mu)=(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)
.
We again set

v_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq e\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(C,\rho,\mu)

noting that this map is a unitary isomorphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y\subseteq X)
with inverse
e^{*}
. It is straightforward to check that
v
is natural.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=(Y_{i})_{i\in I}
be an equivariant big family in
X
(see Definition
2.23
).
Using that
I
is filtered, it is easy to see that we have a wide, possibly non-unital
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear subcategory

\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
with the morphism spaces

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in I}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\,.

(6.5)

Definition 6.7
.

We define the subcategory

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
to be the closure of

\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
with respect to the norm induced from
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.

By construction we have a canonical morphism

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{(-)}\subseteq X)\to\underline{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(I,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, where
\underline{\smash{-}}
stands for forming the constant
I
-diagram. By adjunction
it induces a functor

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)

(6.6)

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
By replacing the subscript
\mathrm{sm}
by
\mathrm{lf}
in the preceeding discussion we get a functor

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\,.

(6.7)

Lemma 6.8
.

The functors in (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We consider the case of (
6.6
). The case of (
6.7
) is analoguous.

For all
i
in
I

the canonical functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

is the identity on the level of objects and it is injective on the spaces of morphisms. The assertion now follows from
[
Bun
, Lem. 4.7]
which provides an explicit description of colimits of this type. ∎

Recall the Definition
3.4
of an ideal inclusion in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

The functors

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\,,\quad\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

are inclusions of ideals.

Proof.

We consider the case with subcript
\mathrm{sm}
. The other case is analogous.

By construction the subcategory

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is wide and its morphism spaces are closed subspaces.
It remains to show that it is an ideal.
Because of the presence of the involution it suffices to verify the condition of a left ideal.

Let
i
be in
I
,
A
be
U
-controlled in
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
, and

B
be in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)
such that
A
and
B
are composable. Then
A\circ B
is in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i^{\prime}}\subseteq X)
, where
i^{\prime}
in
I
is such that
i\leq i^{\prime}
and

U[Y_{i}]\subseteq Y_{i^{\prime}}
. Such an element
i^{\prime}
exists since the family
{\mathcal{Y}}
is assumed to be big, see Condition
2.23
.
3
.

Since
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is dense in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
,

\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
is dense in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
, and

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
is closed in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

we see that
if
A
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
and

B
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
are composable, then

A\circ B\in\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
.
∎

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let

({\mathcal{Y}},Z)
be an equivariant complementary pair on
X
(see Definition
2.25
).
Recall the Definition
3.24
of an excisive square of
C^{*}
-categories.

Lemma 6.10
.

In the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
we will in addition assume that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete. The squares

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}

are excisive.

Proof.

We consider the case with subcript
\mathrm{sm}
. The other case is analogous.

The horizontal functors are inclusions of ideals by
Lemma
6.9
. The right vertical functor is a unital morphism between unital
C^{*}
-categories, and hence the quotients of the horizontal functors are unital and the induced functor

\bar{i}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)

between these quotients is a unital functor.
It remains to verify that this functor
is a unitary equivalence. We define a functor

\bar{p}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)

as follows:

1.

Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
, i.e., an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
Then we choose a representative
(C^{\prime},e)
of the image of
\mu(Z)
. We define
\bar{p}(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)

considered as an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)
. It is here where use the assumption on
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
. In the case of
\mathrm{lf}
the existence of this image already follows from the effective additivity from Assumption
5.1
.

2.

morphisms:
If
[A]
is a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
with representative
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
, then we define

\bar{p}([A])\coloneqq[\tilde{e}^{*}Ae]\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(\tilde{C}^{\prime},\tilde{e}^{*}\tilde{\rho}\tilde{e},\tilde{e}^{*}\tilde{\mu}\tilde{e})\,.

Here
(\tilde{C}^{\prime},\tilde{e})
is the choice for

(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
made in
1
.

We first check that
\bar{p}
is well-defined. Note that

\mu(Y)e=\mu(Y\cap Z)e=ee^{*}\mu(Y\cap Z)e

for
e
as in the construction of
\bar{p}
and any subset
Y
of
X
.
For
i
in
I
the endomorphism
e^{*}\mu(Y_{i}\cap Z)e
of the object
(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)
belongs to the ideal

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)
.

Assume that

A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})

is in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)
for some
i
in
I
.
Then we have the equalities

\tilde{e}^{*}Ae=\tilde{e}^{*}A\mu(Y_{i})e=(\tilde{e}^{*}Ae)(e^{*}\mu(Z\cap Y_{i})e)\,.

This morphism belongs to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)
.

Since the ideal is closed, we conclude that

\tilde{e}^{*}Ae
belongs to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)

for all
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})

in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
.

We now show that
\bar{p}
is compatible with compositions.
Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
and
B\colon(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})\to(\tilde{\tilde{C}},\tilde{\tilde{\rho}},\tilde{\tilde{\mu}})
be morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. For the moment we assume that
A
is
U^{-1}
-controlled for some entourage
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
.
There exists
i
in
I
such that
X\setminus Z\subseteq Y_{i}
. Using that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is big we can choose
i^{\prime}
in
I
such that
U[Y_{i}]\subseteq Y_{i^{\prime}}
. We have the equality

\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}BAe-\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}B\tilde{e}\tilde{e}^{*}Ae=\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}B\tilde{\mu}(X\setminus Z)Ae=(\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}B\tilde{\mu}(X\setminus Z)Ae)(e^{*}\mu(Y_{i^{\prime}}\cap Z)e)\,.

The right-hand side belongs to the ideal
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq Z)
.
Again, since this ideal is closed we can conclude that

\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}BAe-\tilde{\tilde{e}}^{*}B\tilde{e}\tilde{e}^{*}Ae
belongs to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq Z)
for all

A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
and
B\colon(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})\to(\tilde{\tilde{C}},\tilde{\tilde{\rho}},\tilde{\tilde{\mu}})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.

We now construct an isomorphism
u\colon\bar{i}\circ\bar{p}\to{\mathtt{id}}
.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of the quotient
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
, i.e., an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
Then

u_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq[e]\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(C,\rho,\mu)

is a unitary isomorphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
with inverse
[e^{*}]
.
It is straightforward to check that
u
is natural.

Finally, we construct the unitary isomorphism
v\colon\bar{p}\circ\bar{i}\to{\mathtt{id}}
.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)
, i.e., an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)
. Then

v_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq[e]\colon(C^{\prime},e^{*}\rho e,e^{*}\mu e)\to(C,\rho,\mu)

is an isomorphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)/\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)
. It is straightforward to check that
v
is natural.
∎

In contrast to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
the functor
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
has an additional property:

Lemma 6.11
.

The functor
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
is continuous (Definition
2.31
).

Proof.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
and
let
\mathrm{LF}(X)
be the poset of invariant, locally finite subsets of
X
.
We must show that the canonical functor

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{L\in\mathrm{LF}(X)}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(L)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

is an isomorphism.

For every

L
in
\mathrm{LF}(X)
the inclusion
L\to X
induces an inclusion
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(L)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

of full subcategories. By
[
Bun
, Lem. 4.7]
the colimit is given by the union of these subcategories (note that this union is already a closed subcategory).
If
(C,\rho,\mu)
is an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
, then
L\coloneqq{\mathtt{supp}}(C,\rho,\mu)
belongs to
\mathrm{LF}(X)
.
Consequently,
(C,\rho,\mu)
belongs to the image of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(L)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
∎

7
The coarse homology theories
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}

In this section we define the two functors
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}
and show that they are coarse homology theories.

Let
G
be a group and fix
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
. As before we adopt Assumption
5.1
. Then by Definition
5.20
we have the functor and subfunctor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,,\quad\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,.

Let
\mathbf{M}
be an
\infty
-category, and
let
\mathrm{Hg}\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{M}
be a functor.
The following definition introduces some notation.

Definition 7.1
.

1.

We define the functor

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}

as the composition

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Hg}}\mathbf{M}\,.

2.

We define the functor

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}

as the composition

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Hg}}\mathbf{M}\,.

The subscript
c
in
7.1
.
2
indicates continuity, see Theorem
7.3
.
The superscript
G
indicates that the construction uses
G
-objects in a
C^{*}
-category
with strict
G
-action. This should not be confused with the symbol
G
appearing as a subscript in Section
8

which stands for coinvariants, resp. crossed products.

Recall Definition
2.37
of an equivariant coarse homology theory and Definition
3.23
of a finitary homological functor.

Theorem 7.2
.

Assume:

1.

\mathrm{Hg}
is a finitary homological functor.

2.

{\mathbf{C}}
admits countable AV-sums and
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete.

Then

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory.

Proof.

Note that
\mathbf{M}
is stable since it is the target of a homological functor.
We verify the conditions listed in Definition
2.37
.

1.

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is coarsely invariant: Let
f,f^{\prime}
be two parallel morphisms in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
which are close to each other. Then by Lemma
6.2
the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f)
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(f^{\prime})
are unitarily isomorphic.
Since
\mathrm{Hg}
sends unitarily isomorphic functors to equivalent morphisms, the morphisms

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(f)
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(f^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{M}
are equivalent.

2.

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is
u
-continuous:
Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. Then we must show that the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X_{U})\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)

is an equivalence.
It has the following factorization:

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X_{U})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\mathrm{Hg}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hg}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{U}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hg}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\,.

Since
\mathrm{Hg}
is assumed to be finitary it preserves filtered colimits. Therefore
(1)
is an equivalence.
By the Lemma
6.3
the functor
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
is
u
-continuous. Hence
(2)
is also an equivalence.

3.

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is excisive:
Note that
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\emptyset)
is a zero category, see Example
4.7
. Consequently,

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(\emptyset)\simeq 0_{\mathbf{M}}
since
\mathrm{Hg}

is reduced by
[
BEa
, Lem. 13.6.2c]

Let now
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let
({\mathcal{Y}},Z)
be an invariant complementary pair on
X
with

{\mathcal{Y}}=(Y_{i})_{i\in I}
. For every
i
in
I
the morphism

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(Y_{i})\to\mathrm{Hg}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)

is an equivalence by Lemma
6.6
since
\mathrm{Hg}
sends unitarily equivalences to equivalences. By Lemma
6.8

and since
\mathrm{Hg}
preserves filtered colimits we conclude that the canonically induced morphism

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathrm{Hg}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)

is an equivalence, too. Similarly,

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\to\mathrm{Hg}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\subseteq Z)

is an equivalence.
We now consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)}

By Lemma
6.10
the right square is the result of an application of
\mathrm{Hg}
to an excisive square.
It is a cocartesian square, because
\mathrm{Hg}
sends excisive squares to cocartesian squares by
[
BEa
, Lem. 13.6.b]
. We conclude that the outer square is cocartesian.

4.

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
vanishes on flasques: If
X
is flasque, then
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
is a flasque
C^{*}
-category by Lemma
6.4
. By
[
BEa
, Prop. 13.13]
the functor
\mathrm{Hg}
annihilates flasques. We conclude that

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\simeq 0_{\mathbf{M}}
.∎

Recall the Definition
2.31
of continuity.

Theorem 7.3
.

Assume:

1.

\mathrm{Hg}
is a finitary homological functor.

2.

{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive and admits countable AV-sums.

Then

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}
is a continuous equivariant coarse homology theory.

Proof.

The proof that it is an equivariant coarse homology theory is the same as for the Theorem
7.2
. We just use the cases with subscript
\mathrm{lf}
instead of
\mathrm{sm}
in the cited lemmas.

Continuity follows from Lemma
6.11
and the fact that
\mathrm{Hg}
preserves filtered colimits.
∎

Recall Definition
2.40
of a strong equivariant coarse homology theory.

Proposition 7.4
.

The coarse homology theories
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
are strong.

Proof.

We give the argument in the case of
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
. The case of
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is analoguous.

Let
X
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
be weakly flasque (Definition
2.39
) with weak flasqueness implemented by
f\colon X\to X
. Then we apply
\mathrm{Hg}
to the Equation (
6.2
)
obtained in the proof of Lemma
6.4
. Using the additivity of
\mathrm{Hg}
shown in
[
BEa
, Prop. 13.11]
we get

{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)}+\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)(f)\circ\mathrm{Hg}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Hg}(S)

in
{\mathtt{End}}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X))
.
By Condition
2.39
.
1

we have

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)(f)\simeq{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)}
.
The resulting relation

{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)}+\mathrm{Hg}(S)=\mathrm{Hg}(S)
implies that
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\simeq 0
.
∎

Example 7.5
.

We have seen in Theorem
7.3

that the coarse homology theory
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is continuous. In the following example we show that

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}
is not continuous in general. The example is similar to
[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 8.6]
.
For simplicity we consider the case of a trivial group and omit the symbols
G
.
We further assume that the target
\mathbf{M}
of the homological functor
\mathrm{Hg}
is compactly generated.

We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is a
C^{*}
-category which admits countable AV-sums, and which has the properties that
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete and that
\mathrm{Hg}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})\not\simeq 0
(see Example
7.6
), where
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
is the subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}
of unital objects. Unraveling the definitions we have an equivalence
\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(*)\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.

We construct a factorization

\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(*)\xrightarrow{i}\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(*)

of the identity of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(*)
.
The second functor is induced by the projection
{\rm Nature}_{min,max}\to*
in
\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. The first functor
sends
C
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(*)
(we omit the measure since it is a trivial datum) to the object
({\mathbf{C}},\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})
, where
\mu
is the projection-valued measure constructed in
Example
4.6
associated to some previously fixed choice of a free ultrafilter on
{\rm Nature}
.
The functor furthermore acts as the identity on morphisms.

Since
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(*)\simeq\mathrm{Hg}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(*))\not\simeq 0
and
\mathbf{M}
is compactly generated we can find a compact object
M
in
\mathbf{M}
and a morphism
m\colon M\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(*)
such that the composition

M\xrightarrow{m}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(*)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Hg}(i)}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})

is non-trivial. If
F
is any finite subset of
{\rm Nature}
, then we have a
coarsely disjoint partition
(F,{\rm Nature}\setminus F)
of
{\rm Nature}_{min,max}
and
\mu(F)=0
(since the ultrafilter is assumed to be free). By excision we thus have a decomposition

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})\simeq\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(F)\oplus\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max}\setminus F)\,,

and we let
p_{F}\colon\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(F)
be the projection into the first summand.
Since
\mu(F)=0
it follows that
p_{F}\circ\mathrm{Hg}(i)\circ m\simeq 0
.
Since this holds for every finite subset
F
it follows that

\mathrm{Hg}(i)\circ m
is not in the image of

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{F\in\mathrm{LF}({\rm Nature}_{min,max})}\pi_{0}{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{M}}(M,\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(F))\xrightarrow{c}\pi_{0}{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{M}}(M,\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}({\rm Nature}_{min,max}))\,.

On the other hand, if
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}
would be continuous, then the canonical map
c
would be an isomorphism. Hence we would obtain a contradiction.

∎

Example 7.6
.

Here we explain how one can ensure the condition
\mathrm{Hg}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})\not\simeq 0
.
Let
A
be a unital
C^{*}
-algebra and consider the
C^{*}
-category

{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
of very small Hilbert
A
-modules and adjointable compact operators.
This category admits all very small
AV
-sums and its multiplier category
\mathbf{Hilb}(A)
is idempotent complete. Furthermore,

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}=\mathbf{Hilb}(A)^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{proj}}
is the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Hilb}(A)
of finitely generated, projective
A
-modules. The inclusion

A\to\mathbf{Hilb}(A)^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{proj}}
is a Morita equivalence (
[
BEa
, Ex. 16.9]
), where we consider
A
as a
C^{*}
-category with a single object.
If
\mathrm{Hg}
is Morita invariant (see Definition
3.23
), then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{Hg}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Hg}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})
.
So if
\mathrm{Hg}(A)\not\simeq 0
, then also
\mathrm{Hg}({\mathbf{C}}^{u})\not\simeq 0
.
We can apply this to the functor
\mathrm{Hg}\coloneqq\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
and choose for
A
any unital
C^{*}
-algebra with
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{C}^{*}}(A)\not\simeq 0
.

∎

Since
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is a coarse homology theory it sends coarse equivalences to equivalences. In the following we show that under mild additional assumptions it also sends weak coarse equivalences to equivalences (Definition
2.18
).

Recall that for
X
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
we denote by
\mathrm{LF}(X)
the poset of
G
-invariant locally finite subsets of
Y
(Definition
2.30
).

If
L,H
are sets, then a multivalued map
s\colon L\to H
is a subset (also denoted by
s
) of
L\times H
such that the projection
s\to L
is surjective.
The multivalued map
s
is called surjective if the projection
s\to H
is surjective.
For
\ell
in
L
the projection
s(\ell)
of
(\{\ell\}\times H)\cap s
to
H
is called the image of
\ell
under
s
. Note that
s(\ell)
is a subset of
H
.
In order to describe a multivalued map it suffices to describe the non-empty subsets
s(\ell)
for all
\ell
in
L
. For every
h
we consider the subset

L_{h}\coloneqq\{\ell\in L\>|\>h\in s(\ell)\}
of
L
and form
\tilde{L}\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{h\in H}L_{h}
. Then we have maps

L\xleftarrow{q}\tilde{L}\xrightarrow{\tilde{s}}H\,,

(7.1)

where
\tilde{s}
sends the elements of
L_{h}
to
h
for every
h
in
H
and
q
is the natural map.
We say that
s
is proper if
q
and
\tilde{s}
have finite fibres.
If
G
acts on
L
and
H
, then
s
is called equivariant if the subset
s
is
G
-invariant.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and consider
L
in
\mathrm{LF}(X)
and
H
in
\mathrm{LF}(Y)
.

Definition 7.7
.

L
is an
f
-shadow of
H
if there is a proper surjective equivariant multivalued map
s\colon L\to H
such that
(f\times{\mathtt{id}}_{Y})(s)
is a coarse entourage of
Y
.

Definition 7.8
.

\mathrm{LF}(f)
is almost surjective if
every element of
\mathrm{LF}(Y)
admits an
f
-shadow in
\mathrm{LF}(X)
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Assume:

1.

f
is a weak coarse equivalence.

2.

\mathrm{LF}(f)
is almost surjective.

3.

{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums.

4.

\mathrm{Hg}
is Morita invariant.

Then
f\colon\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(Y)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

In view of Assumption
4
it suffices to show that
f_{*}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
is a Morita equivalence, see Definition
3.16
. To this end it suffices to show that
f_{*}
is fully faithful, and that every object in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
is a subobject of an object in the image of
f_{*}
.

We first show that
f_{*}
is fully faithful.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
and
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be objects in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.
Recall that
f_{*}(C,\rho,\mu)=(C,\rho,f_{*}\mu)
.
We have a canonical inclusion

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\subseteq{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)}(f_{*}(C,\rho,\mu),f_{*}(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\,,

where we consider both morphism spaces as closed subspaces of
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}})}^{G}}((C,\rho),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))
.
We have to show that this inclusion is an equality.
To this end we consider a morphism
A
in the space
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)}(f_{*}(C,\rho,\mu),f_{*}(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))
.
Then there exists a family of morphisms
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{C}^{G}}((C,\rho),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))
and a family
(U_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of coarse entourages of
Y
such that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\to\infty}A_{n}=A
in the norm of
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}})}^{G}}((C,\rho),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}))
(equivalently, in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
), and
A_{n}\colon(C,\rho,f_{*}\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},f_{*}\mu^{\prime})
is
U_{n}
-controlled (Definition
5.12
) for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

We fix
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
By Assumption
1
the subset
V_{n}:=(f\times f)^{-1}(U_{n})
of
X\times X
is a coarse entourage of
X
. We show now that
A_{n}\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is
V_{n}
-controlled.
To this end we
consider two subsets
Z,Z^{\prime}
of
X
such that
Z^{\prime}
is
V_{n}
-separated from
Z
.
Then
f(Z^{\prime})
is
U_{n}
-separated from
f(Z)
and hence
f_{*}\mu^{\prime}(f(Z^{\prime}))A_{n}f_{*}\mu(f(Z))=0
since
A_{n}
is
U_{n}
-controlled.
Using
Z\subseteq f^{-1}(f(Z))
and therefore
\mu(Z)=\mu(f^{-1}(f(Z))\mu(Z)
, and analogously for
Z^{\prime}
, we get

\mu^{\prime}(Z^{\prime})A\mu(Z)=\mu^{\prime}(Z^{\prime})\big(f_{*}\mu^{\prime}(f(Z^{\prime}))Af_{*}\mu(f(Z))\big)\mu(Z)=0\,.

This implies that

A\in{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}((C,\rho,\mu),(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))
.

We now show that every object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
is a direct summand of the image of an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
under
f_{*}
.
To this end we fix
(C,\rho,\mu)
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)
. Then
H\coloneqq{\mathtt{supp}}((C,\rho,\mu))
(Definition
5.6
) is an element of
\mathrm{LF}(Y)
by Definition
5.7
.
2
. By Definition
7.7
we can choose an
f
-shadow
L
in
\mathrm{LF}(X)
and a proper surjective equivariant multivalued map
s\colon L\to H
such that

V\coloneqq(f\times{\mathtt{id}}_{Y})(s)

(7.2)

is a coarse entourage of
Y
.

Since
(C,\rho,\mu)
is determined on points (Definition
4.10
)
we can choose images
u_{h}\colon C(h)\to C
in
{\mathbf{C}}
of the projections
\mu(\{h\})
for all
h
in
H
such that
(C,(u_{h})_{h\in H})
represents the AV-sum of the family
(C(h))_{h\in H}
.
Since
(C,\rho,\mu)
is locally finite and hence small we can furthermore conclude that
C(h)
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.

Associated to the multivalued map
s
we construct the maps

L\xleftarrow{q}\tilde{L}\xrightarrow{\tilde{s}}H\,.

as in (
7.1
).
By assumption
q
has finite fibres, and
\tilde{s}
has finite, non-empty fibres.
The group
G
acts on
\tilde{L}
in the canonical way such that
q
and
\tilde{s}
become equivariant.

Since
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small orthogonal AV-sums by Assumption
3

we can choose an orthogonal AV-sum
(C^{\prime},(e_{\tilde{\ell}})_{\tilde{\ell}\in\tilde{L}})
of the family
(C(\tilde{s}(\tilde{\ell})))_{\tilde{\ell}\in\tilde{L}}
.
Since the summands belong to
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
we see that the morphisms
e_{\tilde{\ell}}
belong to
{\mathbf{C}}
for all
\tilde{\ell}
in
\tilde{L}
.

In the following we extend
C^{\prime}
to an object
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. We start with the construction of
\rho^{\prime}
.
Let
g
be in
G
. Then we define

{\rho^{\prime}_{g}}\colon C^{\prime}\to gC^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes for every
\tilde{\ell}
in
\tilde{L}
:

\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{gC^{\prime}}
\textstyle{C(\tilde{s}(\tilde{\ell}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e_{\tilde{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{u_{\tilde{s}(\tilde{\ell})}}
\textstyle{gC(\tilde{s}(g^{-1}\tilde{\ell}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{ge_{g^{-1}\tilde{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{gu_{\tilde{s}(g^{-1}\tilde{\ell})}}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g}}
\textstyle{gC}

(7.3)

In other words, we set

\rho^{\prime}_{g}\coloneqq\sum_{\tilde{\ell}\in\tilde{L}}ge_{g^{-1}\tilde{\ell}}gu_{\tilde{s}(g^{-1}\tilde{\ell})}^{*}\rho_{g}u_{\tilde{s}(\tilde{\ell})}e_{\tilde{\ell}}^{*}\,.

One checks using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
that this sum converges strictly and defines a unitary morphism in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.
Using Diagram (
7.3
) we further check that the cocycle condition
g\rho_{h}\rho_{g}=\rho_{gh}

(see Definition
4.1
.
1
) implies the condition
g\rho_{h}^{\prime}\rho_{g}^{\prime}=\rho^{\prime}_{gh}
for all
g,h
in
G
.
We set
\rho^{\prime}\coloneqq(\rho^{\prime}_{g})_{g\in G}
.

We define the projection-valued measure

\mu^{\prime}
on
C^{\prime}
such that

\mu^{\prime}(\{\ell\})=\sum_{\tilde{\ell}\in q^{-1}(\{\ell\})}e_{\tilde{\ell}}e_{\tilde{\ell}}^{*}\,.

Since
s
is proper these sums are finite, and since the
e_{\tilde{\ell}}
belong
{\mathbf{C}}
also
\mu^{\prime}(\{\ell\})
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}
for every
\ell
in
L
.
Using the explicit formula one checks that

\mu^{\prime}
satisfies the conditions listed Definition
4.3
.
Then
(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.

We now define a morphism

U\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to f_{*}(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})

by

U\coloneqq\sum_{h\in H}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\tilde{s}^{-1}(\{h\})|}}\sum_{\tilde{\ell}\in\tilde{s}^{-1}(\{h\})}e_{\tilde{\ell}}\Big)u^{*}_{h}\ .

Note that the fibres of
\tilde{s}
are non-empty so that the prefactor is well-defined.
In order to see that the sum defining
U
converges strictly we
first observe that

p_{h}\coloneqq\frac{1}{|\tilde{s}^{-1}(\{h\})|}\sum_{\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}\in\tilde{s}^{-1}(\{h\})}e_{\tilde{\ell}}e_{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}}^{*}

is a projection for every
h
in
H
, and that the family
(p_{h})_{h\in H}
is mutually orthogonal. We then argue using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.4.3]
and the involution that the sum over
H
defining
U
converges strictly. Using the explicit formula
we then check using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.4.1]
that
U
is an isometry, and that

gU\rho_{g}=\rho^{\prime}_{g}U
. By construction,
the morphism
U
is
V
-controlled, where
V
is the entourage from (
7.2
).

The isometry
U
exhibits
(C,\rho,\mu)
as a subobject of
f_{*}(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.
∎

Example 7.10
.

The map
G_{max,max}\to*
is a weak coarse equivalence. If
G
is infinite we have

\mathrm{LF}(G_{max,max})=\emptyset
, but
\mathrm{LF}(*)
is not empty.
Then in this case

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(G_{max,max})\simeq 0
, but in general
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(*)\simeq{\mathrm{Hg}}({\mathbf{C}}^{u,G})\not\simeq 0
, where
{\mathbf{C}}^{u,G}
denotes that category of
G
-objects in the category of unital objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.
This shows the relevance of Condition
7.9
.
2
.

∎

In order to check the Condition
7.9
.
2
the following lemma is sometimes helpful.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.

Lemma 7.11
.

Assume:

1.

f
is a weak coarse equivalence.

2.

There exists an entourage
U
of
Y
such that for every point
y
in
Y
there is
x
in
X
with:

(a)

f(x)\in U[\{y\}]
.

(b)

G_{x}\subseteq G_{y}
, where
G_{x}
and
G_{y}
denote the stabilizers of
x
and
y
.

(c)

|G_{y}/G_{x}|<\infty
.

Then
\mathrm{LF}(f)
is almost surjective.

Proof.

After enlarging
U
if necessary can assume that
U
is
G
-invariant.
Let
H
be in
\mathrm{LF}(Y)
.
We fix a representative
h
for every
[h]
in
H/G
. Then we can find
\ell_{h}
in
X
such that

(f(\ell_{h}),h)\in U
,
G_{\ell_{h}}\subseteq G_{h}
and
|G_{h}/G_{\ell_{h}}|<\infty
. We set
L\coloneqq\bigcup_{[h]\in H/G}G\ell_{h}
and
define the
G
-invariant subset

s\coloneqq\bigcup_{[h]\in H/G}G(\ell_{h},h)
of
L\times H
. Then
s
is a
G
-equivariant surjective multivalued map and

(f\times{\mathtt{id}}_{Y})(s)\subseteq U
.

We have
\tilde{L}\cong\bigsqcup_{[h]\in H/G}G\ell_{h}
. For every
[h]
in
H/G
we have
|\tilde{s}^{-1}(h)|=|G_{h}/G_{\ell_{h}}|<\infty
. Thus by
G
-invariance all fibres of
\tilde{s}
are finite.

Let
\ell
be in
L
.
If
g\ell_{h}\in q^{-1}(\ell)
, then
gh\in U^{-1}[f(\{\ell\})]
.
Since
H
is locally finite and
U^{-1}[f(\{\ell\})]
is a bounded subset of
Y
the intersection

H\cap U^{-1}[f(\{\ell\})]
is finite. Since

q^{-1}(\ell)\subseteq\tilde{s}^{-1}(H\cap U^{-1}[f(\{\ell\})])

and
the fibres of
\tilde{s}
are finite we conclude that the fibres of
q
are finite, too.
Hence
s
is proper.

It remains to show that
L
is locally finite.
Let
B
be a bounded subset of
X
. Since
f
is a coarse equivalence, it is bornological and

U^{-1}[f(B)]
is bounded in
Y
.
Since
H
is locally finite,
H\cap U^{-1}[f(B)]
is finite. Since
\tilde{s}
has finite fibres and
\tilde{s}(q^{-1}(L\cap B))\subseteq H\cap U^{-1}[f(B)]
, we conclude that
L\cap B
is finite.
∎

Example 7.12
.

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let
U
be an invariant coarse entourage. Then

P_{U}(X)
is defined as the
G
-simplicial set of finitely supported probability measures on
X
with
U
-bounded support. We consider
P_{U}(X)

as a
G
-bornological coarse space with the coarse structure induced by the path quasi-metric coming from the spherical path metric on the simplices, and with the bornology generated by the subcomplexes

P_{U}(B)
for the bounded subsets
B
of
X
.

We consider the canonical inclusion

f\colon X\to P_{U}(X)
which sends
x
in
X
to the
\delta
-measure at
x
.
This inclusion satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
7.11
.

Let us make Proposition
7.9
explicit in this case for the functor
\mathrm{Hg}=\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
from (
3.2
).
Note that
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
is a Morita invariant, finitary homological functor by
[
BEa
, Thms. 14.4 & 16.17]
.
We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and admits all very small AV-sums.
Then by Proposition
7.9

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)\to\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(P_{U}(X))

(7.4)

is
an equivalence for every invariant entourage
U
of
X
.

This can be applied in order to define the equivariant coarse assembly map for
X
by the naive equivariant generalization of
[
BE20a
, Defn. 9.7]
by

\displaystyle\mu_{X}\colon\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(X)))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\partial}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}}{\Sigma}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(P_{U}(X))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\simeq}}\Sigma\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X)\,,

where the equivalences (
7.4
) are used in order to get the marked equivalence, and

\partial
is the boundary map of the cone sequence
[
BEKW20b
, Cor. 9.30]
.

By Proposition
7.4
the equivariant coarse homology theory
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is strong. Using
[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 11.3]
we see that

X\mapsto\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G}}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(X)))

gives rise to an equivariant coarse homology theory, and that the assembly map refines to a natural transformation between equivariant coarse homology theories

\mu\colon\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\to\Sigma\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\,.

This is the analogue of
[
BEKW20b
, Cor. 11.26]
, but
without having to twist by a bornological coarse space with a free
G
-action (denoted by
Q
in the reference).

∎

Example 7.13
.

In
[
BE20b
, Sec. 8.6]
the coarse
K
-homology
K\!\mathcal{X}\colon\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{Sp}
was defined as the composition
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}\circ{\mathbf{C}}^{*}
, where the functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\colon\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
sends
X
in
\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to the Roe category
{\mathbf{C}}^{*}(X)
as introduced in
[
BE20b
, Defn. 8.74]
. In this example we explain how this construction fits into the framework of the present paper.

We work in the case of the trivial group and therefore in the following drop all the notation referring to the group action. For
{\mathbf{C}}
we
take
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
of very small Hilbert spaces and
compact operators. It admits all very small AV-sums.
We will show that there is an equivalence of coarse homology theories

K\!\mathcal{X}\simeq\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

This will follow from an equality of functors

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}={\mathbf{C}}^{*}\colon\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,.

We first compare the objects. In both cases an object
(C,\mu)
on
X
in
\mathbf{BornCoarse}
consists of a Hilbert space
C
together with a projection valued, finitely additive measure
\mu
. For both sides we require that
C
is the orthogonal sum of the images of the family
(\mu(\{x\}))_{x\in X}
. Here it is important that classical orthogonal sums of Hilbert spaces in
\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})
coincide with AV-sums in
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
by
[
BEa
, Thm. 8.4]
.

For
{\mathbf{C}}^{*}(X)
we in addition require that
\mu(B)H
is finite-dimensional for every
bounded subset
B
of
X
. This condition is equivalent to the condition that

\mu(B)\in{\mathbf{C}}
required for
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. For
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
we in addition require that
{\mathtt{supp}}(C,\mu)
is locally finite. Since
\mu(B)
is finite-dimensional and every point of the support contributes non-trivially to the dimension the set
{\mathtt{supp}}(C,\mu)\cap B
is indeed finite for any bounded subset
B
of
X
.

In both cases the morphism spaces are defined as the closures of the spaces of bounded operators of controlled propagation. In order to get an actual equality of morphism spaces, we use

\mathbf{Hilb}({\mathbb{C}})
as a concrete model of the multiplier
category
\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
(this is possible by
[
BEa
, Lem. 8.1]
) in the construction of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
.

Finally, the functoriality for morphisms in
\mathbf{BornCoarse}
is defined in the same way for

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{*}(X)
.

∎

8
The coarse homology theories
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c,G}

In this section we introduce two further coarse homology theories
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c,G}
. They are the analogues of the colimit theories considered in
[
BCKW
]
which are an important ingredient in the proof of an isomorphism result for the assembly map
[
BKW21
]
.

From (
5.5
) and (
5.6
)
in the case of the trivial group we obtain functors

\mathbf{BornCoarse}\times C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{ndeg}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,,\quad(X,{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto\mathbf{\bar{C}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)

(8.1)

and

\mathbf{BornCoarse}\times C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{eadd,ndeg}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,,\quad(X,{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)

(8.2)

For a group
G

we have a fully faithful functor

G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BornCoarse})

which sends a
G
-bornological coarse space to the underlying bornological coarse space with the
G
-action by automorphisms.
This functor just forgets the condition that
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{X}
must be cofinal in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
(see Example
2.5
). In formulas we will not write this functor explicitly.

We now fix
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and adopt Assumption
5.1
. By composition we get the functors

\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BornCoarse})\xrightarrow{\eqref{adsvoihqjoi3rffsdadfc}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})

(8.3)

and

\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,\mathbf{BornCoarse})\xrightarrow{\eqref{adsvoihqjoi3rffsdadfcccc}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\,.

(8.4)

We now use the maximal crossed product functor

-\rtimes G\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}

from
[
Bun
, Defn. 5.9]
and its restriction to unital
C^{*}
-categories.

Definition 8.1
.

1.

We define the functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}\coloneqq\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(-)\rtimes G\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\ .

2.

We define the functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}\coloneqq\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}(-)\rtimes G\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,.

We use the maximal crossed product since it has the necessary exactness properties needed for the verification of the axioms for a coarse homology theory below.
It should not be confused with the reduced crossed product

[
BEa
, Sec. 12]
which occurs in the present paper in the statement of Proposition
9.2
.
3
below.

Recall the Definition
2.18
.
2
of a weak coarse equivalence.

Lemma 8.2
.

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
send weak coarse equivalences to unitary equivalences.

Proof.

We give the argument in the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
. The case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
is analoguous.
If
X\to X^{\prime}
is a weak coarse equivalence, then by the
Lemma
6.2
the morphism
\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)\to\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
is a weak equivalence in the sense of
[
Bun
, Defn. 7.6]
, i.e., a unitary equivalence after forgetting the
G
-action. Furthermore, by
[
Bun
, Prop. 7.9]
the functor
-\rtimes G
sends weak equivalences to unitary equivalences. Consequently,

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X^{\prime})
is a unitary equivalence.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

The functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
and
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
are
u
-continuous.

Proof.

We give the argument in the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
. The case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
is analoguous.
First of all note that colimits in functor categories are calculated objectwise.
Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.
By Lemma
6.3
and since
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}
is cofinal in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
(see Definition
2.1
.
4
) we get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X_{U})\cong\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)

(8.5)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.
Applying
-\rtimes G
gives

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X_{U}))\rtimes G\cong\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)\rtimes G\,.

It remains to show that the canonical functor

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}(\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X_{U})\rtimes G)\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}}\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X_{U}))\rtimes G

(8.6)

is an isomorphism. This follows from
[
BELb
, Prop. 7.25]

which states that the maximal crossed product functor preserves filtered colimits of systems whose structure maps are injective on objects. In our case the structure maps are identities.
∎

Let
X
be in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let
({\mathcal{Y}},Z)
be a complementary pair.

Lemma 8.4
.

We have a diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}(({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\subseteq Z)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}

(8.7)

and, provided
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete, also a diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(({\mathcal{Y}}\cap Z)\subseteq Z)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(X)}

(8.8)

where the left horizontal maps are filtered colimits of unitary equivalences and the right squares are excisive.

Proof.

We give the argument in the case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
. The case of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
is analoguous.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=(Y_{i})_{i\in I}
.
For every
i
in
I
we have a diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i}\cap Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}((Y_{i}\cap Z)\subseteq Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
. The left horizontal morphisms are weak equivalences by Lemma
6.6
.
We apply the functor
-\rtimes G
and get

\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i}\cap Z)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}((Y_{i}\cap Z)\subseteq Z)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Z)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i})\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(Y_{i}\subseteq X)\rtimes G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\tilde{\bar{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{sm}}(X)\rtimes G}

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Since the crossed product sends weak equivalences to unitary equivalences by
[
Bun
, Prop. 7.9]
, the left horizontal functors are unitary equivalences.
We now form the colimit over
i
in
I
and use the notation
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm},G}^{\mathrm{ctr}}
at the outer corners.
Note that the evaluation of a functor on a big family is by definition the colimit of its values on the members.
We furthermore use that
I
is filtered in order to remove the colimit symbol for the constant
I
-diagrams in the right part.
We obtain the diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.7179pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.7179pt\raise
