where
\tilde{e}
is the canonical embedding of the summand with index
Y
.

One now checks in a straightforward manner that

w^{*}(C,\phi,\rho)=(w^{*}C,w^{*}\phi,w^{*}\rho)\,.

is a well-defined object in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(W)
.

(b)

morphisms: Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)
.
Then we let
w^{*}(A)\colon w^{*}C\to w^{*}C^{\prime}
be the morphism which is uniquely determined by the family

(A_{w(Y)})_{Y\in\pi_{0}(W)}
.
We now explain why
w^{*}A
is well-defined. First of all note that
(A_{B})_{B\in\pi_{0}(Z)}
is bounded and equivariant.
We conclude that
(A_{w(Y)})_{Y\in\pi_{0}(W)}
is also bounded. Since
w
is equivariant we furthermore see that
(A_{w(Y)})_{Y\in\pi_{0}(W)}
is also equivariant.
There exists a family
(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of morphisms
A_{n}\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{C}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)
and a family
(U_{n})_{\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{Z}
such that

A_{n}
is
U_{n}
-controlled for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
, and
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\to\infty}A_{n}=A
in
{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,C^{\prime})
.
Then
(A_{n,w(Y)})_{Y\in\pi_{0}(W)}
is
U_{\pi_{0}(W)}\cap(w\times w)^{-1}(U_{n})
-controlled for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.
By Definition
10.5
.
2
we have
U_{\pi_{0}(W)}\cap(w\times w)^{-1}(U_{n})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{W}
, and furthermore

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\to\infty}\sup_{Y\in\pi_{0}(W)}\|A_{n,w(Y)}-A_{w(Y)}\|=0
.
By the discussion above we see that
A
is controlled.
We have the following formula:

w^{*}A=\sum_{B\in\pi_{0}(W)}e^{\prime,w^{*}}_{B}u^{C^{\prime},*}_{w(B)}Au_{w(B)}^{C}e^{w^{*},*}_{B}\,.

(10.7)

One checks in a straightforward manner that
w^{*}
is compatible with compositions and the involution.

3.

We consider two composable bounded coverings
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w}
\textstyle{Z}
. We construct the
2
-isomorphism

a_{v,w}\colon(wv)^{*}\Rightarrow v^{*}w^{*}

as follows.
Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z)
.
Then we define

a_{v,w}(C,\rho,\mu)\colon((wv)^{*}C,(wv)^{*}\phi,(wv)^{*}\rho)\to(v^{*}w^{*}C,v^{*}w^{*}\phi,v^{*}w^{*}\rho)

by

a_{v,w}(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq\sum_{B\in\pi_{0}(V)}e_{B}^{v^{*}}u_{v(B)}^{w^{*}C,*}e_{v(B)}^{w^{*}}e_{B}^{(wv)^{*},*}\,.

(10.8)

One checks by a calculation that
a_{v,w}(C,\rho,\mu)
is a unitary multiplier isomorphism. One furthermore checks that

a_{v,w}\coloneqq(a_{v,w}(C,\rho,\mu))_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(Z))}

is a natural unitary multiplier isomorphism.

4.

We now consider an admissible square

\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{w}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{Z}

Then must define the transformation

b_{g,u}\colon f_{*}\circ w^{*}\Rightarrow u^{*}\circ g_{*}\,.

Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(V)
.
Then we define

b_{g,u}(C,\rho,\mu)\colon f_{*}w^{*}(C,\rho,\mu)\to u^{*}g_{*}(C,\rho,\mu)

by

b_{g,u}(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq\sum_{B\in\pi_{0}(U)}e^{u^{*}}_{B}u^{g_{*}C,*}_{u(B)}\sum_{A\in\pi_{0}(f^{-1}(B))}u^{C}_{w(A)}e_{A}^{w^{*},*}\,.

(10.9)

One checks by a calculation that

b_{g,u}(C,\rho,\mu)
is a unitary multiplier isomorphism.

We must verify that the
functors and
2
-isomorphisms constructed above satisfy the seven conditions listed above. All these conditions are verified by straightforward (but lengthy) calculations. These calculations start with inserting the explicit definitions of the transformations given above and then
proceed by employing the orthogonality relations for the families of canonical isometries. ∎

Let
\mathrm{Hg}\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\to\mathbf{M}
be a functor to an
\infty
-category
\mathbf{M}
.

Theorem 10.7
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums and
\mathrm{Hg}
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences, then the composition

\mathrm{Hg}\circ\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}

admits transfers.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of
[
BEKW20c
, Prop. 2.63]
combined with the discussion around the diagram in
[
BEKW20a
, (3.8)]
.

We consider
\mathrm{Hg}
as a functor
{\mathtt{N}}(\mathrm{Hg})\colon{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\to\mathbf{M}
.

As explained in
[
Del12
]
or
[
Bun19
, Thm. 1.4]
the category
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
has a simplicial model category structure whose weak equivalences
W
are the unitary equivalences.
Applying the coherent nerve functor we obtain an
\infty
-category
{\mathtt{N}}^{\mathrm{coh}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.
Then we have a canonical inclusion functor
{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\to{\mathtt{N}}^{\mathrm{coh}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.
This inclusion is an explicit model of the
Dwyer–Kan localization

\ell\colon{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\to{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})[W^{-1}]
.

We now construct the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(\mathrm{Hg})}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{(1)}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})[W^{-1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(2)}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathrm{Hg}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}\,.}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}^{\mathrm{coh}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}_{2}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(3)}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Hg}_{2,1}}

(10.10)

The morphism
(1)
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences. Consequently we obtain the morphism
(2)
and the filler of the corresponding triangle from the universal property of the Dwyer–Kan localization
\ell
.
As explained above the morphism (2) is an equivalence. The morphism (3) is actually an isomorphism of simplicial sets.

Since
\mathrm{Hg}
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences
we obtain the factorization
\tilde{\mathrm{Hg}}
and the upper triangle from the universal property of the Dwyer–Kan localization
\ell
.
We now define
\mathrm{Hg}_{2,1}\coloneqq\tilde{\mathrm{Hg}}\circ(2)^{-1}\circ(3)
. The diagram (
10.10
) provides an equivalence of functors

{\mathtt{N}}(\mathrm{Hg})\simeq\mathrm{Hg}_{2,1}\circ\ell_{2}\colon{\mathtt{N}}({\mathbf{Cat}})\to\mathbf{M}\,.

This equivalence provides the right triangle in the following diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(G\mathbf{BornCoarse})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}})}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{2}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{N}}(\mathrm{Hg})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{M}\,.}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{BornCoarse}_{{\mathtt{tr}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf},\mathrm{tr}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{N}}_{2}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Hg}_{1,2}}

(10.11)

The existence of the left square is exactly the statement of Theorem
10.2
.
The outer part of the diagram (
10.11
) exhibits the required extension of
\mathrm{Hg}\circ\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
.
∎

11
Induction from and restriction to subgroups

Let
G
be a group and consider a subgroup
H
.
We consider a coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})

and assume that it is effectively additive.
Then we can form the functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}

(Definition
5.20
.
2
).
Furthermore, we can restrict the
G
-action on
{\mathbf{C}}
to an
H
-action (this will not be written explicitly) and define the functor

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}\colon H\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\,.

We have an induction functor

{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\colon H\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\,,

(11.1)

see
[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 6]
(it will be recalled in the proof of Proposition
11.1
).

For the following statement we implicitly compose the functors
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
and

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}
with the functor
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
, where

C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
is the
(2,1)
-category of
C^{*}
-categories, functors and unitary isomorphisms.

Proposition 11.1
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits AV-sums of size
|H\backslash G|
, then we have a natural equivalence

\phi\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}

of functors from
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to the
(2,1)
-category
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
.

Remark 11.2
.

The reason for considering the target of the two functors in the
(2,1)
-category is that the transformation
\phi
which we will construct is only natural for
X
in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
up to unique isomorphisms which are in general not equalities. This is because in the construction we must choose objects representing images of projections, and these choices are only determined up to unique unitary isomorphisms, see also Remark
11.3
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

We start with recalling the construction of the induction functor (
11.1
) from
[
BEKW20b
, Sec. 6]
.
Let
X
be in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. We define
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X)
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
as follows.

•

The underlying
G
-set of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X)
is
G\times_{H}X
with the left-action of
G
on the left factor. Here
G\times_{H}X
is the quotient of the set
G\times X
by
H
with respect to the action given by
h(g,x)=(gh^{-1},hx)
.

•

The bornology on
G\times_{H}X
is generated by the images under the natural projection
G\times X\to G\times_{H}X
of the subsets
\{g\}\times B
for all bounded subsets
B
of
X
.

•

The coarse structure is generated by the images of
{\mathtt{diag}}_{G}\times U
under this projection for all entourages
U
of
X
.

The functor (
11.1
)
is now defined as follows:

•

objects: The functor
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}
sends
X
in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X)
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.

•

morphisms: The functor
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}
sends a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to the morphism in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
given by the map
G\times_{H}f\colon G\times_{H}X\to G\times_{H}Y
of underlying sets induced from
{\mathtt{id}}_{G}\times f\colon G\times X\to G\times Y
.

For
X
in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
we now construct a morphism

\phi_{X}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\,.

1.

objects: Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))
.
We have a
H
-equivariant inclusion
i\colon X\to G\times_{H}X
given by

x\mapsto[e,x]
. Here
[g,x]
denotes the image in
G\times_{H}X
of
(g,x)
under the natural projection
G\times X\to G\times_{H}X
. Since
{\mathbf{C}}
is effectively additive and
(C,\rho,\mu)
is determined on points we can choose an image
(\tilde{C},u)
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
of the projection
\mu(i(X))
on
C
.
Then we define
\tilde{\rho}=(\tilde{\rho}_{h})_{h\in H}
by
\tilde{\rho}_{h}\coloneqq(hu^{*})\rho_{h}u\colon\tilde{C}\to h\tilde{C}
and

\tilde{\mu}\colon\mathcal{P}(X)\to\mathrm{Proj}(\tilde{C})
by
\tilde{\mu}(Y)\coloneqq u^{*}\mu(Y)u
. The functor
\phi_{X}
sends an object
(C,\rho,\mu)
to the object
(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. One checks the conditions listed in Definitions
4.3
and
5.7
by straightforward calculations and thus verifies that the object
(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
is well-defined.

2.

morphisms: If
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))
, then
the functor
\phi_{X}
sends
A
to
u^{\prime,*}Au\colon(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})\to(\tilde{C}^{\prime},\tilde{\rho}^{\prime},\tilde{\mu}^{\prime})
.
One checks that this is a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
and that this construction is compatible with the involution and composition. Here it is important that
i(X)
is a union of coarse components of
G\times_{H}X
and therefore
A
does not “propagate” between
i(X)
and its complement.

We now argue that
\phi_{X}
is a unitary equivalence.
We first see using the
G
-equivariance of morphisms in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))

and the fact that the
G
-translates of
i(X)
form a coarsely disjoint partition of
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X)

that
\phi_{X}
is bijective on morphism spaces. This immediately implies that
\phi_{X}
is fully faithful.
It remains to argue that
\phi_{X}
is essentially surjective. Let

(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
be given. We choose a section
s\colon H\backslash G\to G
of the projection
G\to H\backslash G
such that
s([e])=e
.
Then we choose an orthogonal AV-sum
(C,(e_{[k]})_{[k]\in H\backslash G})
in
{\mathbf{C}}
of the family of objects
(s(k)\tilde{C})_{[k]\in H\backslash G}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
.
It is at this point where we use the additional assumption on
{\mathbf{C}}
.
We then observe using
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
that there exist a unique extension of
the
H
-cocycle
\tilde{\rho}
on
\tilde{C}
to a
G
-cocycle
\rho
on
C

such that
he^{*}_{[e]}\rho_{h}e_{[e]}=\tilde{\rho}_{h}
for all
h
in
H
. Furthermore,
there exists a unique extension of

\tilde{\mu}
to a
G
-equivariant
\mathrm{Proj}(C)
-valued measure on
G\times_{H}X
such that for any subset
Y
of
X
we have

\tilde{\mu}(Y)=e_{[e]}^{*}\mu(i(Y))e_{[e]}
.
Applying
\phi_{X}
to the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))
we get an object which is unitarily isomorphic to the original object
(\tilde{C},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\mu})
.

Let
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
be a morphism in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, and let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be in

\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))
. In the construction of
\phi_{X}
we have chosen an image
(\tilde{C},u)
of
\mu(i(X))
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
. Similarly, in the construction of

\phi_{X^{\prime}}
applied to
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*}(C,\rho,\mu)
we have chosen an image
(\tilde{C}^{\prime},u^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
of the projection
{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*}\mu(i^{\prime}(X^{\prime}))
on
C
. We now note that
\mu(i(X))={\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*}\mu(i^{\prime}(X^{\prime}))
.
Consequently, there exists a unique unitary multiplier isomorphism
v_{f,(C,\rho,\mu)}\colon\tilde{C}\to\tilde{C}^{\prime}
such that
v_{f,(C,\rho,\mu)}u=u^{\prime}
.
The family

v_{f}\coloneqq(v_{f,(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X)))}

is a unitary isomorphism of functors
v_{f}\colon f_{*}\phi_{X}\to\phi_{X^{\prime}}{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*}
filling the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Rightarrow,v_{f}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(X^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(X^{\prime})}

If
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime}
is a second morphism in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
, then using the uniqueness of the choices of the unitary isomorphisms
v_{f,(C,\rho,\mu)}
, etc. one checks that

(v_{f^{\prime}}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(f)_{*})(f^{\prime}_{*}\circ v_{f})=v_{f^{\prime}f}\,.\qed

Remark 11.3
.

One could explicitly strictify the functor
\phi
to a natural transformation of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
-valued functors as follows. In a first step one chooses for every object
C
in
{\mathbf{C}}
and every projection
p
on
C
a representative
(\tilde{C},u)
of the image of
p
.
In the construction of
\phi_{X}
we then use this choice for the mage of
\mu(i(X))
.

If we proceed in this way, then the transformations
v_{f}
for morphisms
f
in
H\mathbf{BornCoarse}
all become identities.

∎

Let

\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to H\mathbf{BornCoarse}

be the functor which sends a
G
-bornological coarse space to the
H
-bornological coarse space obtained by restriction of the action from
G
to
H
.
We have a canonical natural transformation

\psi\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}

(11.2)

of functors from
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
. Its evaluation
\psi_{X}
on
X
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
is given as follows:

1.

objects: The functor
\psi_{X}
sends the object
(C,\rho,\mu)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
with
\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
to the object
(C,\rho_{|H},\mu)
of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X))
, where

\rho_{|H}\coloneqq(\rho_{h})_{h\in H}
.

2.

morphisms: The functor
\psi_{X}
is the canonical inclusion of
G
-invariant into
H
-invariant morphisms.

Assume that
X
is in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. Then we have an isomorphism

c_{X}\colon{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X))\to(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes X

in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}

given by the map of underlying sets

G\times_{H}X\to G/H\times X\,,\quad[g,x]\mapsto(g,gx)\,.

The family

(c_{X})_{X\in G\mathbf{BornCoarse}}
is a natural isomorphism of functors

c:{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}\to(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes-\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\,.

(11.3)

We note that the projection
p_{X}\colon(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes X\to X
is a bounded covering (Definition
10.5
).
The family
p\colon(p_{X})_{X\in G\mathbf{BornCoarse}}
is a natural transformation of functors

p\colon(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes\iota\to\iota\ ,

(11.4)

where
\iota\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to G\mathbf{BornCoarse}_{{\mathtt{tr}}}
is as in (
10.1
).

Recall that
{\mathbf{C}}
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums. Then the pull-back
p_{X}^{*}
is defined by Theorem
10.2
.
We consider the composition

\displaystyle\mathclap{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\xrightarrow{p_{X}^{*}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}((G/H)_{min,min}\otimes X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c_{X,*}^{-1}}}{{\cong}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X)))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{{\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X)}}}}{{\simeq}}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X))}

which we will denote by
\tilde{\psi}_{X}
. The family

\tilde{\psi}\coloneqq(\tilde{\psi}_{X})_{X\in G\mathbf{BornCoarse}}
is a natural transformation

\tilde{\psi}\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}

(11.5)

of functors from
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
to
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{2,1}
.
We must again consider values in the
(2,1)
-category
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
since the naturality of
\tilde{\psi}
is only up to canonical unitary isomorphisms instead of equalities. Even if we would strictify
\phi
as in Remark
11.3

we would still get non-trivial fillers of the naturality squares from the
2
-categorial nature of
p
.

The following proposition compares
\psi
in (
11.2
) with
\tilde{\psi}
in (
11.5
).

Proposition 11.4
.

There exists a unitary isomorphism
u\colon\psi\to\tilde{\psi}
of natural transformations from
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
to
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}
.

Proof.

For
X
in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
we must construct a unitary isomorphism

u_{X}\colon\psi_{X}\to\tilde{\psi}_{X}\,.

Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)
. Then we must construct an isomorphism

u_{X,(C,\rho,\mu)}\colon\psi_{X}(C,\rho,\mu)\to\tilde{\psi}_{X}(C,\rho,\mu)\,.

In order to avoid lengthy formulas we explain the object
\tilde{\psi}_{X}(C,\rho,\mu)
in words. In the first step of the construction of
p_{X}^{*}
in the proof of Theorem
10.2
for every coarse component
Y
of
X
we have chosen an image

(C(Y),u_{Y})
of
\mu(Y)
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.
By Lemma
4.13
the object
C
is isomorphic to the orthogonal AV-sum of the family
(C(Y))_{Y\in\pi_{0}(X)}
. The underlying object of
p_{X}^{*}(C,\rho,\mu)
is
given by an orthogonal AV-sum over the objects
C(p_{X}(Z))
indexed by the set of coarse components
Z
of
(G/H)_{min,min}\otimes X
. The subsum over the components in
[e]\times X
is canonically unitarily isomorphic in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
to
C
. Since
c_{X}^{-1}([e]\times X)=i(X)
we conclude, using the details of the construction of
\phi_{X}
in the proof of Proposition
11.1
, that the underlying object of
\tilde{\psi}_{X}(C,\rho,\mu)
is canonically unitarily isomorphic to

C
.
We then check that this isomorphism is compatible with the cocycles and measures and yields the desired unitary isomorphism
u_{X,(C,\rho,\mu)}
.

In all cases above the canonical unitary isomorphisms are determined uniquely by their compatibilty with the structure maps of the chosen images of projections.

One then checks that the family

u_{X}\coloneqq(u_{X,(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}})}
is a unitary isomorphism of functors.

Let
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
be a morphism in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.
Then from the
2
-categorial naturality of
(p_{X}^{*})
and
\phi

we get a unitary isomorphism

v_{f}\colon\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(f)_{*}\tilde{\psi}_{X}\to\tilde{\psi}_{X^{\prime}}f_{*}\,.

Then one finally checks, using the uniqueness of the unitary isomorphisms going into the constructions of
u_{X}
and
v_{f}
, that the composition

\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\psi}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{\Downarrow u_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(f)_{*}}
\textstyle{\Downarrow v_{f}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\psi}_{X^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\Downarrow u_{X^{\prime}}^{-1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{H,\mathrm{ctr}}(\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}(X^{\prime}))}

of isomorphisms of functors is the identity.
∎

Let
\mathrm{Hg}\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{M}
be a finitary homological functor.

Corollary 11.5
.

Assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums.

1.

We have a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{H}_{c}
of
H
-equivariant coarse homology theories.

2.

We have a natural transformation
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\to\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{H}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}
of
G
-equivariant coarse homology theories induced by
\psi
in (
11.2
).

Proof.

We use Theorem
7.3
in order to conclude that

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}
and
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{H}
are
G
- and
H
-equivariant coarse homology theories, respectively.
The composition
\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}
is an
H
-equivariant coarse homology theory by
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 4.19]
(which settles the universal case).
The natural equivalence in Assertion
1
is obtained by applying the extension
{\mathrm{Hg}}_{1,2}
(see (
10.11
)) to the transformation
\phi
obtained in Proposition
11.1
.
∎

Remark 11.6
.

Assume that
E^{G}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}
is a
G
-equivariant coarse homology theory. Then by
[
BEKW20d
, Lem. 4.19]
we can define an
H
-equivariant coarse homology theory by

E^{G}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}:H\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}
.

If
E^{G}
admits transfers, then we can furthermore define a natural transformation

E^{G}\to E^{G}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}

(11.6)

as the composition

E^{G}\xrightarrow{p^{*}\circ c_{*}^{-1}}E^{G}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}\,,

where
p
and
c
are as in (
11.4
) and (
11.3
).

For

{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
admitting all very small AV-sums
we have
the two equivariant coarse homology theories

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
and

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{H}_{c}
which are defined independently from each other. By Corollary
11.5
.
1

we know that the transformation
\phi
provides an equivalence of

H
-equivariant coarse homology theories

\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}_{H}^{G}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Hg}(\phi),\simeq}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{H}_{c}\,.

(11.7)

Consequently, we can consider the natural transformation

r\colon\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\xrightarrow{\eqref{3rfjoifqwefqwfqwf}}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\circ{\mathtt{Ind}}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ewfqwfewfwqfqwef4}}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{Hg}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{H}_{c}\circ\mathtt{Res}^{G}_{H}\,.

(11.8)

The combination of Proposition
11.4
and Corollary
11.5
.
2
then says that
the abstract transformation (
11.8
)
is induced by the obvious inclusion of
C^{*}
-categories
\psi
in (
11.2
). This fact will be further employed in
[
BL
]
.

∎

12
Strong additivity of
K{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}

Recall from Definition
2.36
the notion of a strongly additive functor on
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
.
Recall that the topological
K
-theory functor (see
[
BEa
, Def. 14.3]
)

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

is a finitary homological functor
[
BEa
, Thm. 14.4]
. For the purpose of the present section its main additional property is
that it preserves arbitrary very small products of finitely additive
C^{*}
-categories
[
BEa
, Thm. 15.7]
.

Assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is countably and effectively additive.
By Theorem
7.3
we have a continuous equivariant coarse homology theory

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

Theorem 12.1
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums, then the functor
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}_{c}^{G}
is strongly additive.

The proof of Theorem
12.1
will be given further below. We first start with the corresponding additivity statement for the functor
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}
.

Let
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
be a very small family in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. Recall Definition
2.35
of the free union
\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}
of this family. For
i
in
I
let
f_{i}\colon X_{i}\to\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}
denote the inclusion.
In the formulation of the following lemma the symbol
0
stands for a functor which sends every object to a zero object.

Lemma 12.2
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small AV-sums, then there exists a unitary equivalence

\phi\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)\to\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
such that for all
i,j
in
I
we have a unitary isomorphism

\mathrm{pr}_{j}\circ\phi\circ f_{i,*}\cong\begin{cases}{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})}&\text{if }i=j\,,\\
0&\text{if }j\not=i\,.\end{cases}

(12.1)

Proof.

We will construct functors

\phi\colon\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)\to\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})\,,\quad\psi\colon\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})\to\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)

and then show that they are inverse to each other up to unitary isomorphism.

We start with the construction of
\phi
.

1.

objects: Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
. By Lemma
4.12
for every
i
in
I
we choose an image
(C_{i},u_{i})
of the projection
\mu(X_{i})
. We further define
\rho_{i}=(\rho_{g,X_{i}})_{g\in G}
as in (
10.5
), where
\rho_{g,X_{i}}\colon C_{i}\to gC_{i}
(since
X_{i}
is
G
-invariant), and
\mu_{i}\coloneqq\mu_{X_{i}}
by (
10.6
). In this way we get an object

\phi(C,\rho,\mu)\coloneqq((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}

of
\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
.

2.

morphisms: Let
A\colon(C,\rho,\mu)\to(C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
. Note that
the subsets
X_{i}
of the free union are mutually coarsely disjoint. Therefore
A
does not propagate between different components.
For every
i
in
I
we consider the restriction
A_{i}\coloneqq u_{i}^{*}Au_{i}
of
A
to
C_{i}
. Then we have
\sup_{i\in I}\|A_{i}\|=\|A\|
. We define the morphism
\phi(A)
in
\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
by

\phi(A)\coloneqq(A_{i})_{i\in I}\colon((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}\to((C^{\prime}_{i},\rho^{\prime}_{i},\mu^{\prime}_{i}))_{i\in I}\,.

One checks in a straightforward manner that this construction defines a functor
\phi
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
.
Note that it depends on the choices made for the images of the projection
\mu
.

We now define the functor
\psi
.

1.

objects: Let
((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}
be an object of
\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
. Since we assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all very small orthogonal AV-sums we can choose an orthogonal AV-sum

(\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i},(e_{i})_{i\in I})
. Using

[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
we obtaine the last two entries of the right-hand side in

\psi(((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I})\coloneqq(\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i},\oplus_{i\in I}\rho_{i},\oplus_{i\in I}\mu_{i})\,.

One checks again in a straightforward manner that this object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
is well-defined.

2.

morphisms: Let

(A_{i})_{i\in I}\colon((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}\to((C^{\prime}_{i},\rho^{\prime}_{i},\mu^{\prime}_{i}))_{i\in I}

be a morphism in

\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{sm}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
. Since morphisms in a
product of
C^{*}
-categories are precisely the uniformly bounded families of morphisms
we have
\sup_{i\in I}\|A_{i}\|<\infty
and can use
[
BEa
, Lem. 7.8]
to define
a morphism

\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}\colon\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i}\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}C^{\prime}_{i}

in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
.
One checks that it is compatible with the cocycles, i.e.,
\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
is a morphism in
{(\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}})}^{G}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
.
It remains to show that it is a morphism in the Roe category. Fix
\varepsilon
in
(0,\infty)
. Then for every
i
in
I
there exists an entourage
U_{i}
of
X_{i}
and a
U_{i}
-controlled operator
A_{i}^{\prime}\colon(C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i})\to(C^{\prime}_{i},\rho_{i}^{\prime},\mu_{i}^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
such that
\|A_{i}-A_{i}^{\prime}\|\leq\varepsilon
. Then
\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}^{\prime}
is in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
. It is controlled by the entourage
\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
in the coarse structure of the free union, and we have
\|\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}-\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}^{\prime}\|\leq\varepsilon
. Since we can choose
\varepsilon
arbitrary small we conclude that

\oplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
.

One checks in a straightforward manner that this construction defines a functor
\psi
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}
. It depends on the choices involved in the sums.

We now show that
\phi
and
\psi
are mutually inverse to each other.

1.

The unitary
u\colon\phi\circ\psi\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathtt{id}}
: Let
((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}
be an object of
\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
.
For every
j
in
I
let
((\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i})_{j},u^{\prime}_{j})
be the choice of an image of
(\oplus_{i\in I}\mu_{i})(X_{j})
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
adopted in the construction of
\phi
. We define
the multiplier morphism

a_{j}\coloneqq e_{j}^{*}u^{\prime}_{j}\colon(\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i})_{j}\to C_{j}\,.

We get a unitary multiplier

u_{((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}}\coloneqq(a_{j})_{j\in I}\colon((\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i})_{j})_{j\in I}\to(C_{j})_{j\in I}\,.

The family
(u_{((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}})_{((C_{i},\rho_{i},\mu_{i}))_{i\in I}\in\mathrm{Ob}(\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i}))}

is the desired unitary isomorphism of functors.

2.

The unitary
v\colon\psi\circ\phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{id}}
: Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object of
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})
.
We use Lemma
4.13
in order to conclude that

v_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq\sum_{i\in I}u_{i}e_{i}^{*}\colon\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i}\to C\,.

is a unitary multiplier.
Then the family
(v_{(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}))}
is the desired unitary isomorphism of functors.

We now show that
\phi
has the asserted property (
12.1
).
Let
i
be in
I
. We construct the unitary isomorphism

w\colon\mathrm{pr}_{i}\circ\phi\circ f_{i,*}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathtt{id}}_{\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})}

as follows. Let
(C,\rho,\mu)
be an object in
\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i})
. Then the multiplier

u_{i}\colon(f_{*}C)_{i}\to C
is a unitary, where
((f_{*}C)_{i},u_{i})
denotes the choice of the image
of
(f_{i,*}\mu)(X_{i})
in
\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{C}}
involved in the construction of
\phi
.
We set
w_{(C,\rho,\mu)}\coloneqq u_{i}
.
The family
(w_{(C,\rho,\mu)})_{(C,\rho,\mu)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i}))}
is the desired unitary isomorphism.

If
i,j
are in
I
and
i\not=j
, then
(f_{i,*}C)_{j}=0
. Consequently,
\mathrm{pr}_{j}\circ\phi\circ f_{i,*}\cong 0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12.1
.

Since
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is excisive, it is
\pi_{0}
-excisive (Definition
2.33
).
Let
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family in
G\mathbf{BornCoarse}
. For
j
in
J
we consider the invariant coarsely disjoint partition

(X_{j},\bigcup_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}X_{i})
of
\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}
.
Since
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
is
\pi_{0}
-excisive we get a decomposition

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)\simeq\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X_{j})\oplus\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}X_{i}\big)\,.

We let

p_{j}\colon\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)\to\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X_{j})

be the corresponding projection onto the first summand.
We must show that

(p_{j})_{j\in J}\colon\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}\big(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}\big)\to\prod_{j\in I}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X_{j})

is an equivalence.
We consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p_{i})_{i\in J}}
defn.
\textstyle{\prod_{i\in I}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}(X_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
defn.
\textstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(\bigsqcup^{\mathrm{free}}_{i\in I}X_{i}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\simeq,\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\phi)}
Lemma

12.2

\textstyle{\prod_{i\in I}\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{i}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{k}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\prod_{j\in I}\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{j}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\mathrm{pr}_{k})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{!}
\textstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{j}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(f_{j,*})}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{jk}{\mathtt{id}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}(\mathbf{\bar{C}}_{\mathrm{lf}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X_{k}))}

where the marked equivalence together with commutativity of the right triangle for every
k
in
I
are stated in
[
BEa
, Thm. 15.7]
, and commutativity of the lower left square is asserted by (
12.1
). The big outer square commutes for every
j,k
in
J
in view of the construction of the map
p_{k}
. This implies that the middle pentagon commutes as well.
This finally implies the assertion of the theorem.
∎

13
CP-functors

Let
G
be a group and let
G\mathbf{Orb}
denote its orbit category.
In this section we describe a functor
G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}
associated to
{\mathbf{C}}
in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
admitting all very small AV-sums.
The main theorem states that this functor is a CP-functor.

Since
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
is in particular finitely additive we have the functor

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]\rtimes_{r}G\colon G{\mathbf{Set}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}

from (
9.10
).
We can consider the functor

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}^{u}[-]\rtimes_{r}G)\colon G{\mathbf{Set}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\,.

(13.1)

Definition 13.1
.

We define
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}_{G,r}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}
to be the restriction of (
13.1
) to the orbit category.

Before we can state the main theorem about
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}_{G,r}
we must recall the notion of a CP-functor.
We have the functor

i\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\,,\quad S\mapsto S_{min,max}

(see Example
2.12
).
We consider a functor

M\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{M}\,.

Definition 13.2

(
[
BEKW20d
, Defn. 1.8]
)
.

M
is a CP-functor if

1.

\mathbf{M}
is stable, admits all very small colimits and limits, and is compactly generated.

2.

There exists an equivariant coarse homology theory
E\colon G\mathbf{BornCoarse}\to\mathbf{M}
(see Definition
2.37
)
such that:

(a)

M
is equivalent to
E_{G_{can,min}}\circ i
(see Examples
2.38
&
2.13
).

(b)

i.

E
is continuous (Definition
2.31
).

ii.

E
is strongly additive (Definition
2.36
).

iii.

E
admits transfers (Definition
10.1
).

Theorem 13.3
.

If
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
admits all very small AV-sums, then the functor
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}_{G,r}
is a CP-functor.

Proof.

The target category of the functor
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}_{G,r}
is the large category
\mathbf{Sp}
of small spectra. This category is stable, compactly generated and admits all small colimits and limits. This verifies Condition
13.2
.
1
.

For
E
in Definition
13.2
we take the equivariant coarse homology theory

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
obtained by specializing Definition
7.1
.
2
to the case
\mathrm{Hg}=\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
.

By applying
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
to the equivalence stated in Proposition
9.2
.
3
we obtain an equivalence

\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c,G_{can,{min}}}\circ i\simeq\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}_{G,r}\,.

This verifies Condition
13.2
.
2a
.

The coarse homology theory
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{c}
has the properties required in
13.2
.
2b
:

1.

It is continuous by Theorem
7.3
.

2.

It is strongly additive by Theorem
12.1
.

3.

It admits transfers by Theorem
10.7
which is applicable for
\mathrm{Hg}=\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
since
\mathrm{K^{C^{*}Cat}}
, being a homological functor, sends unitary equivalences to equivalences.∎
